DECLARATION OF RAYMOND Y. KIM 
1. I am principal of the law firm of Ray Kim Law, APC and counsel for 
plaintiff Roberto Sandoval Carrillo (“Plaintiff”) in this matter.  I submit this 
declaration in support of Plaintiff’s Motion for Class Certification.  I have personal 
knowledge of the facts set forth in this declaration. 
2. For fifteen years I practiced in “big law” and am a former partner of 
Holland & Knight LLP.  I have nineteen years of experience in the consumer 
protection arena.  I also have seventeen years of experience in consumer class actions 
and litigated my first class action on behalf of Capital One Auto Finance in 2009.   
3. I seek to be appointed as class counsel in this action.  I will continue to 
vigorously prosecute this action until completion and will commit all resources 
necessary to represent the Megan’s Law Class, Fair Chance Class, and Fair Credit 
Reporting Act (“FCRA”) Class. 
4. I have substantial experience in all aspects of litigation ranging from pre-
litigation, pleadings, discovery, depositions, motion practice, and some trial 
experience. 
5. I am familiar with all aspects of class action litigation, including post 
settlement/judgment administration of class action settlement funds and have served 
as the primary liaison and supervising attorney overseeing the administration of many 
class action settlements.  I have been directly involved in the design and 
implementation of numerous class action settlements.  A brief sample of my class 
action experience includes, but is not limited to, the following matters:  Sanders v. 
RBS Citizens, N.A., No. 13-cv-03136-BAS-RBB, 2021 U.S. Dist. LEXIS 22565 (S.D. 
Cal. Feb. 5, 2021); Wright v. Nationstar Mortg. LLC, No. 14 C 10457, 2016 U.S. Dist. 
LEXIS 115729 (N.D. Ill. Aug. 29, 2016); Lee v. Winslow & Shoomaker, LLC, et al. 
(Case No. BC705954); Debra Johnson, et al. v. Hyundai Capital America d/b/a Kia 
Motors Finance (LASC, Case No. BC565263 (the “Action”); Davidson v.  United 
Auto Credit Corporation (E.D. Va., 1:20-cv-01263-LMB-JFA).

6. I have been personally involved in all aspects of this case since its 
inception to the filing of this Motion.  I conducted the underlying investigation and 
assessment of Plaintiff’s individual and class claims, prepared all aspects of the 
pleadings, and handled all discovery, including discovery disputes, and two informal 
discovery conferences with Judge Karen E. Scott, and depositions.   
7. In my judgment, proposed class representative Roberto Sandoval Carrillo 
has diligently accepted his responsibility and easily meets the adequacy and typicality 
threshold.  Mr. Sandoval Carrillo has taken the role of lead class representative very 
seriously, and I continue to have regular, thorough, and thoughtful discussions with 
him regarding the claims set forth in this case.  
8. I have communicated by phone, Zoom, and email with Mr. Sandoval 
Carrillo countless times during the pendency of this case.  I have discussed various 
aspects of this case with him, prepared him for his deposition, and defended his 
deposition.  I worked extensively with Mr. Sandoval Carrillo to prepare his discovery 
responses and extensive document production of nearly six hundred (600) pages of 
communications, including hundreds of pages of emails with Mr. Sandoval Carrillo’s 
probation officer, and the declaration in support of this motion for class certification.  
He has, without exception, been entirely responsive to all communications from my 
office and has unselfishly devoted a significant amount of time for the prosecution of 
this case.  What is more, given Mr. Sandoval Carrillo’s paralegal certificate and legal 
background, he has been tremendously helpful analyzing the factual and legal issues 
presented in this case. 
9. On July 23, 2025, I caused to be served written discovery on defendant 
The Vons Companies, Inc. (“Vons”), including Requests for Production (“RFPs”), Set 
No. 1, Interrogatories, Set No. 1, and Requests for Admission (“RFAs”), Set No. 1.  A 
true and correct copy of the RFPs, Set One, Interrogatories, Set One, and RFAs, Set 
One, are attached as Exhibits 1, 2 and 3, respectively. 
10. On October 29, 2025, I took the Rule 30(b)(6) deposition of Toni North,

11. In response to Plaintiff’s written discovery, Vons repeatedly served

emails and meetings with Vons’ counsel and two informal discovery conferences with 
Judge Karen E. Scott, on November 21, 2025 Vons served its Second Supplemental

Exhibit 6.    
12. On November 21, 2025 Vons also served its Second Supplemental 
Responses to Plaintiff’s RFPs, Set No. One and produced an updated excel 
spreadsheet containing class data (“Class Data”).  Vons had produced a different 
version of the Class Data on October 10, 2025, with a significant amount of 
information redacted.  I have not had an opportunity to depose Vons on the 
differences contained in its November 21 Class Data production.  A true and correct 
copy of Vons’ Second Supplemental Responses to Plaintiff’s RFPs, Set No. One is 
attached as Exhibit 7.  See Exhibit 6, at Second Supplemental Responses to Plaintiff’s 
RFPs, Set No. One, at No. 15.  
13. Ms. North’s deposition testimony and Vons’ discovery responses and 
production support granting certification of the Megan’s Law Class, Fair Chance 
Class, and FCRA Class.

information from the Megan’s Law site (“SOR information”) for employment 
purposes.  During her deposition, Ms. North testified that Vons “partially relied” on 
SOR information:

See Exhibit 4, Toni North Dep., at 52:3-25; 54:2-25. 
15. Plaintiff’s Interrogatory No. 8 asks, “State the total number of PERSONS 
in California who applied for employment with YOU and were denied employment 
(e.g. no employment offer given, conditional offer of employment rescinded, 
employment terminated) as a result of information from the MEGAN’S LAW 
registry.”  In its Second Supplemental Response, Vons responded, inter alia, “There 
are approximately 43 persons in California through September 2025 who appeared on 
the Megan’s Law Website and who applied for employment, went through the 
background check process, and were denied employment with Responding Party, 
which includes Vons and Pavilions banner stores.”  See Exhibit 6, at Second 
Supplemental Response to Interrogatories, No. 8 (emphasis added). 
16. In addition, the Class Data produced by Vons shows there are at least 
forty-three (43) job applicants who applied for a job with Vons and were denied as a 
result of SOR information found in their background reports.  The Class Data contains 
a column of information (Column I) entitled, “Highest Crime.”  This “Highest Crime” 
column lists various convictions, including one entitled “Sex Offender.”  The Class 
Data shows that from 2021 to September 2025 there are at least 43 applicants whose 
“Highest Crime” was listed as “Sex Offender.”  The Class Data shows that all of these 
applicants were “Failed,” meaning they were not hired or the conditional offer was

From 2021 to September 2025 there are at least fifty-six (56) applicants who are

18. Vons sent these Megans Law Class members the substantively same 
Adverse Action Notice as Plaintiff.  These Adverse Action Notices stated Vons was 
withdrawing employment “based on” the applicant’s status as a “Sex Offender” 
disclosed in the background report.  On March 7 Plaintiff received an Adverse Action 
Notice from Vons and Asurint stating that Vons is withdrawing Plaintiff’s 
employment offer “based on” Plaintiff’s SOR information and the decision was 
“influenced” by the SOR information: 
Dear Applicant, 
 
We are writing to inform you that your conditional offer of 
employment, transfer or promotion with Vons/Pavilions has been 
withdrawn.   
 
This decision was influenced in whole or in part by a pre-
employment screening report prepared by Asurint. Please note 
that Asurint did not make this employment decision on behalf of 
our company. This decision was made pursuant to Vons/Pavilions 
hiring policies. 
 
We have identified the following convictions on your report to be 
job-related:

Fair Chance Class 
20. Plaintiff’s Interrogatory No. 6 asks, “State the total number of PERSONS 
in California who applied for employment with YOU and were denied employment 
(e.g. no employment offer given, conditional offer of employment rescinded, 
employment terminated) as a result of their conviction history.”  In its Second 
Supplemental Response, Vons responded, inter alia, “There are approximately 901 
persons in California through September 2025 with conviction histories who applied 
for employment, went through the background check process, and were denied 
employment with Responding Party, which includes Vons and Pavilions banner 
stores.”  See Exhibit 6, Second Supplemental Response to Interrogatories, at No. 6 
(emphasis added). 
21. Despite Plaintiff’s RFPs, Interrogatories and RFAs, and Plaintiff’s 
discovery disputes with Vons (including two informal discovery conferences with 
Judge Karen E. Scott), Vons has produced no records showing it conducted an

individualized assessment of Plaintiff or any of the other 900 applicants who were

22. Vons only produced a single document from Albertsons, entitled 
“Standard Operating Procedures Regarding Use and Review of Retail Job Applicants’ 
Criminal Conviction Histories – California” (“Standard Operating Procedures”).  A 
true and correct copy of the Standard Operating Procedures are attached as Exhibit 
10.  These Albertsons’ Standard Operating Procedures do not show that Vons 
conducted any individualized assessments, for Plaintiff or any Fair Chance Class 
members.  Indeed, Vons’ reliance on Albertsons’ Standard Operating Procedures is 
misplaced and the Procedures are irrelevant here.  Vons has gone on record stating 
that it is not affiliated with Albertsons for purposes of hiring.  In response to 
Plaintiff’s RFPs, Set No. 2, Vons responded to each of the RFPs as follows: 
Responding Party does not employ employees of Albertsons 
Companies, Inc., Albertsons, Safeway Inc., or Andronico’s.  Moreover, 
the putative class alleged in Plaintiff’s complaint only seeks to include 
employees of The Vons Companies, Inc.  Therefore, information 
related to Albertsons Companies, Albertsons, Safeway Inc., or 
Andronico’s is irrelevant and not reasonably calculated to lead to the 
discovery of admissible evidence.  
Attached as Exhibit 8 is a true and correct copy of Vons’ Response to Plaintiff’s 
Requests for Production, Set No. 2, RFP Nos. 36-51.  
23. In addition, in an email Vons’ counsel also stated, “Albertsons, Safeway, 
and Andronicos are not associated with The Vons Companies, Inc.  The Vons 
Companies, Inc. is the entity Mr. Carrillo was hired by and is the entity sued.  He was 
never employed by the Albertsons Companies, Safeway, or Andronicos.”  Thus, 
Vons’ production of Albertsons’ Standard Operating Procedures does not show it 
conducted individualized assessments of Plaintiff or the 900 other members of the Fair 
Chance Class.  Attached as Exhibit 9 is a true and correct copy of Vons’ counsel’s

24. In addition, in response to Plaintiff’s RFP No. 17 seeking class 
documents evidencing individualized assessment conducted by Vons, Vons responded 
that “Responding Party does not have responsive documents other than the types of 
documents identified in its supplemental response to Request for Production 14 and 
will not provide that information as it is overbroad and seeks merits-based discovery.”  
See Exhibit 7, Vons’ Second Supplemental Responses to Plaintiff’s RFPs, Set No. 
One, Supplemental Response to RFP No. 17.  In RFP No. 14, Plaintiff sought 
documents relating to Vons’ “individualized assessment” of Plaintiff.  In response to 
RFP No. 14, Vons merely produced Plaintiff’s post-conditional offer questionnaire, 
Pre-Adverse Action Notice and Adverse Action Notice, and Plaintiff’s mitigating 
evidence submitted in response to the Pre-Adverse Action Notice.  See Exhibit 7, 
Vons’ Second Supplemental Responses to Plaintiff’s RFPs, Set No. One, at Vons’ 
Response and Supplemental Response to RFP No. 14.  But Vons’ responded that it 
was not in possession, custody, or control of any other records relating to other 
applicants evidencing individualized assessments.  See Exhibit 7, Vons’ Second 
Supplemental Responses to Plaintiff’s RFPs, Set No. One, at Vons’ Response and 
Supplemental Response to RFP No. 17.  Plaintiff raised this issue with the Judge 
Karen E. Scott at the first IDC, and the Court ordered Vons to respond that no 
responsive documents exist reflecting individualized assessments of class members.  
See Court’s IDC Order No. 1, Dkt. No. 42, ¶ 6 (“With respect to RFP No. 17, Vons 
shall serve a supplemental response by no later than Friday, October 10, 2025, 
stating that Vons conducted a reasonable and diligent search and no responsive 
documents exist with respect to other putative class members, other than the 
documents identified in response to RFP No. 14 with respect to Plaintiff.”) (bold in 
original).  Vons did just that.  See Exhibit 7, Vons’ Second Supplemental Responses 
to Plaintiff’s RFPs, Set No. One, at Vons’ Supplemental Response to RFP No. 17.

901 Fair Chance Class members.  Pursuant to the Class Data, Vons denied 
employment to all applicants with convictions. There is no evidence in the Class Data 
showing that Vons conducted any individualized assessment of Plaintiff or anyone 
else, such as assessing: (i) whether the crime or harm was to property or people; (ii) 
the degree of the harm (e.g., amount of loss in theft); (iii) the permanence of the harm; 
(iv) the context in which the offense occurred; (v) whether a disability, including but 
not limited to a past drug addiction or mental impairment, played a role; (vi) the 
amount of time passed; (vii) the specific job duties; or (viii) whether the harm (e.g. 
possession of images of minors) is likely to occur in the workplace (e.g. behind a deli 
counter).  Based on the Class Data and evidence Vons produced, Vons simply has a 
blanket practice of denying employment to those with a conviction history.  
FCRA Class 
26. Plaintiff’s Interrogatory No. 8 asks, “State the total number of PERSONS 
in California who applied for employment with YOU and were denied employment 
(e.g. no employment offer given, conditional offer of employment rescinded, 
employment terminated) as a result of information from the MEGAN’S LAW 
registry.”  In its Second Supplemental Response, Vons responded, inter alia, “There 
are approximately 43 persons in California through September 2025 who appeared on 
the Megan’s Law Website and who applied for employment, went through the 
background check process, and were denied employment with Responding Party, 
which includes Vons and Pavilions banner stores.”  See Exhibit 6, at Second 
Supplemental Response to Interrogatories, at No. 8 (emphasis added). 
27. Each of these 43 persons are members of the FCRA Class because they 
applied for employment with Vons, Asurint conducted a background search and 
furnished a consumer report containing SOR on them, and Vons used the background 
report for employment decisions, namely withdrawing conditional offers of 
employment and denying employment.  Whether or not Asurint’s provision of 
background reports to Vons is a violation of the FCRA is a merits issue that is

28. In fact, the Services Agreement produced by Vons governing the 
relationship between Vons and Asurint, expressly states, “[Asurint] shall not return 
Sex Offender Registry information in the states of California and Nevada.”  The 
Agreement was signed in 2011.  However, in 2012 an Addendum was signed which 
provided that Asurint will provide sex offender registry information at $400.00 per 
background check.     
 
I declare under penalty of perjury under the laws of California and the United 
States that the foregoing is true and correct. 
 
 
Signed this 14th day of January 2026.

DEFINITIONS 
 Unless the context indicates otherwise,  the following words and phrases are 
defined and used herein as follows:

communication of any information by a consumer reporting agency bearing on a 
consumer’s credit worthiness, credit standing, credit capacity, character, general 
reputation, personal characteristics, or mode of living which is used or expected to be 
used or collected in whole or in part for the purpose of serving as a factor in 
establishing the consumer’s eligibility for credit or insurance to be used primarily for 
personal, family, or household purposes; employment purposes; or another purpose 
authorized by Section 1681b of the federal Fair Credit Reporting Act.  CONSUMER 
REPORT also means an investigate consumer report as defined in Cal. Civ. Code § 
1786.2.

AMENDED NOTICE OF DEPOSITION OF THE PERSON MOST KNOWLEDGEABLE FOR THE VONS 
COMPANIES, INC. 
TO ALL PARTIES AND THEIR RESPECTIVE ATTORNEYS OF RECORD: 
Pursuant to Federal Rule of Civil Procedure 30(b)(6), please take notice that 
plaintiff Roberto Sandoval Carrillo (“Plaintiff”), though his counsel of record, will 
take the deposition of the person most knowledgeable of THE VONS COMPANIES, 
INC. (“Vons”) on October 29, 2025, at 9:00 a.m.  This deposition will be taken 
pursuant to FRCP 30 et seq. The deposition shall take place virtually via Zoom 
videoconference. The deposition will be taken before a duly certified shorthand 
reporter and will continue thereafter day-to-day, Saturdays, Sundays and statutory 
holidays excepted, until completed. Additionally, this deposition is intended for 
possible use at trial. 
Examination of the designated agent(s) or representative(s) will be directed to, 
but not limited, the person most knowledgeable on the following matters: 
DEFINITIONS 
1. “ASURINT” refers to defendant One Source Technology LLC d/b/a 
Asurint (“Asurint”) or anyone acting on behalf of Asurint, including but not limited to 
the employees, agents, representatives, and attorneys of Asurint.   
2.  “CARRILLO” or “Plaintiff” refers to plaintiff Roberto Sandoval 
Carrillo. 
3. “COMMUNICATION” means any transmission or exchange of 
information, statements, ideas, thought or sentiment between or among two or more 
PERSONS, orally or in writing, and include any conversation or discussion, whether 
face-to-face or by means of a telephone, telegraph, telex, telecopier, facsimile 
transmission, letter, e-mail, electronic or other medium.  
4. “CONSUMER REPORT” means any written, oral, or other 
communication of any information by a consumer reporting agency bearing on a 
consumer’s credit worthiness, credit standing, credit capacity, character, general 
reputation, personal characteristics, or mode of living which is used or expected to be 
used or collected in whole or in part for the purpose of serving as a factor in

personal, family, or household purposes; employment purposes; or another purpose 
authorized by Section 1681b of the federal Fair Credit Reporting Act.  CONSUMER 
REPORT also means an “investigative consumer report” as defined in Cal. Civ. Code 
§ 1786.2. 
5. “DOCUMENTS” means written, printed, typed, or visually or orally 
reproduced material of any kind, whether or not privileged, including, but not limited 
to, all letters, emails, correspondence, contracts, agreements, bills, orders, receipts, 
records, books, computer tapes and printouts, memoranda, notes, notebooks, maps, 
sketches, cablegrams, telegrams, reports, press releases, advertising and promotional 
literature, prints, drawings, plans, photographs, printed forms, manuals, brochures, 
lists, publications, videotapes, other tape recordings, films, microfilm, electronic 
media and all other writings, including drafts, typings, printings, minutes or copies or 
reproductions thereof in the possession, custody, or control of YOU. 
6. “FAIR CHANCE ACT” means the California Fair Chance Act, 
Government Code § 12952. 
7. “MEGAN’S LAW” means California Megan’s Law, Penal Code § 
290.46 (“Megan’s Law”), which prohibits the use of information from the Megan’s 
Law Website from being used for employment purposes. 
8. “PERSON(S)” mean natural persons as well as other entities, including 
but not limited to businesses, corporations, associations, partnerships, firms, 
organizations, and governmental (municipal, state, or federal) agencies or bodies.

recording, or referring in any way, directly or indirectly, to the subject matter of the 
Request, as well as all DOCUMENTS and/or COMMUNICATIONS underlying, 
supporting, attached or appended to such DOCUMENTS and/or 
COMMUNICATIONS.

PRELIMINARY STATEMENT 
The following answers are based upon info rmation presently known and available 
to Defendant.  Discovery, both internal and external, is still ongoing, and Defendant 
reserves the right to supplement these answer s with subsequently di scovered information 
and/or to introduce such information at trial.  Each answer is subject to all objections as to 
competence, relevance, materiality, propri ety, admissibility, a nd any and all other 
objections or grounds that would require exclusion of the answers produced by Defendant, 
or any part thereof, if any of these answer s were presented in court.  All appropriate 
objections and grounds are here by reserved and may be inter posed at trial regarding the 
introduction into evidence of an answer produced by Defendant herein. 
The fact that Defendant has responded to or  objected to any interrogatory or part 
thereof may not be taken as an admission that they admit the existence of any fact set forth 
in or assumed by such interrogatory or that such answer constitutes relevant evidence other 
than as expressly admitted herein.  No im plied admissions whatsoever are intended by 
these answers.  The fact that Defendant has answered part or all of any interrogatory shall 
not be construed to be a waiv er by Defendant of any objecti ons to part or all of any 
interrogatory. 
To the extent that part or all of any interrogatory calls fo r information which 
constitutes material prepared in anticipation of litigation or for trial, information or material 
covered by the work-product doc trine or information protected from disclosure by virtue 
of a privilege including, but not limited to, the attorney-client communication privilege, 
Responding Party objects to each and every su ch interrogatory and will not supply or 
render any information or material protected from discovery by virtue of the work-product

Plaintiff was hired for the Deli Clerk positi on at Vons in San Diego, California in 
2023 due to an urgent operational ne ed for deli clerks at the stor e. At the time of hire, the 
store was experiencing a staffing shortage that  impacted its ability to maintain service 
levels and meet customer demand. As a result,  Plaintiff was hired prior to the completion 
of a background check, consistent with the company’s practice of extending conditional 
offers of employment subject to satisfactory completion of pre-employment screening. The 
information considered at the time of hire in cluded Plaintiff’s application materials, prior 
work experience in food service, and his interview. Hiring decisions are generally made by 
the Store Director.

California in 2023 due to an urgent operational need for deli clerks at the store. At the time 
of hire, the store was experiencing a staffing shortage that impacted its ability to maintain 
service levels and meet customer demand. As  a result, Plaintiff was hired prior to the 
completion of a background check, consistent with the company’s practice of extending 
conditional offers of employment subject to  satisfactory completion of pre-employment 
screening. His continued employment was contingent upon successful completion of a 
background check. After Plaintiff had worked for a period of time, the results of his 
background check were received and revealed that Plaintiff had a conviction requiring 
registration as a sex offender. In accordanc e with California Gove rnment Code section 
12952, the Responding Party conducted an individualized assessment to determine 
whether Plaintiff’s conviction history had a direct and adverse relationship with the specific 
duties of the Deli Clerk position. Plaintiff wa s provided written notice of the preliminary 
decision to rescind his employment offer, along with a copy of the conviction history 
report, and an opportunity to respond with  evidence of rehabilitation or mitigating 
circumstances. After considering all information submitted, the Responding Party made a 
final decision to terminate Plaintiff’s em ployment based on the conclusion that his 
conviction history had a direct and adverse re lationship to the duties of the Deli Clerk 
position, including but not limited to interaction with minors. The individuals involved in

independently determined that Plaintiff’s conviction history had a direct and adverse 
relationship with the specific duties of the Deli Clerk position.

TO PLAINTIFF ROBERTO SANDOVAL CARRILLO AND HIS ATTORNEYS OF 
RECORD: 
Pursuant to Federal Rule of Civil Proced ure 34, Defendant The Vons Companies, 
Inc. (hereinafter “Defendant”) hereby provides responses to the Requests for Production of 
Documents, Set One, served upon it by Plaintiff Roberto Sandoval Carrillo (“Plaintiff”) as 
follows: 
PRELIMINARY STATEMENT 
The following answers are based upon info rmation presently known and available 
to Defendant.  Discovery, both internal and external, is still ongoing, and Defendant 
reserves the right to supplement these answer s with subsequently di scovered information 
and/or to introduce such information at trial.  Each answer is subject to all objections as to 
competence, relevance, materiality, propri ety, admissibility, a nd any and all other 
objections or grounds that would require exclusion of the answers produced by Defendant, 
or any part thereof, if any of these answer s were presented in court.  All appropriate 
objections and grounds are here by reserved and may be inter posed at trial regarding the 
introduction into evidence of an answer produced by Defendant herein. 
The fact that Defendant has responded to or  objected to any request or part thereof 
may not be taken as an admission that it admits  the existence of any fact set forth in or 
assumed by such request or that such answer  constitutes relevant evidence other than as 
expressly admitted herein.  No implied admissions whatsoever are intended by these 
answers.  The fact that Defendant has answer ed part or all of any request shall not be 
construed to be a waiver by Defendant of any objections to part or all of any request. 
To the extent that part or all of any re quest calls for information which constitutes 
material prepared in anticipation of litigation or  for trial, information or material covered 
by the work-product doctrine or information protected from disclosure by virtue of a 
privilege including, but not limited to, th e attorney-client communication privilege, 
Responding Party objects to each and every such request and will not supply or render any 
information or material protected from discovery by virtue of the work-product doctrine or

PRELIMINARY STATEMENT 
The following answers are based upon info rmation presently known and available 
to Defendant.  Discovery, both internal and external, is still ongoing, and Defendant 
reserves the right to supplement these answer s with subsequently di scovered information 
and/or to introduce such information at trial.  Each answer is subject to all objections as to 
competence, relevance, materiality, propri ety, admissibility, a nd any and all other 
objections or grounds that would require exclusion of the answers produced by Defendant, 
or any part thereof, if any of these answer s were presented in court.  All appropriate 
objections and grounds are here by reserved and may be inter posed at trial regarding the 
introduction into evidence of an answer produced by Defendant herein. 
The fact that Defendant has responded to or  objected to any request or part thereof 
may not be taken as an admission that they adm it the existence of any fact set forth in or 
assumed by such request or that such answer  constitutes relevant evidence other than as 
expressly admitted herein.  No implied admissions whatsoever are intended by these 
answers.  The fact that Defendant has answer ed part or all of any request shall not be 
construed to be a waiver by Defendant of any objections to part or all of any request. 
To the extent that part or all of any re quest calls for information which constitutes 
material prepared in anticipation of litigation or  for trial, information or material covered 
by the work product doctrine or  information protected from disclosure by virtue of a 
privilege including, but not limited to, th e attorney-client communication privilege, 
Defendant objects to each and every such reque st and will not supply or render any 
information or material protected from discovery by virtue of the work-product doctrine or

Standard Operating Procedures Regarding Use and Review of Retail Job 
Applicants’ Criminal Conviction Histories - California 
This document sets forth standard operating procedures to be implemented in 
adjudicating criminal conviction histories for retail job applicants in order to ensure 
compliance with California law. 
(1) The Company currently conducts criminal background checks on retail job 
applicants after a conditional offer of employment has been made.   
a. Prior to making a conditional offer of employment, no application for 
employment shall include any question that seeks the disclosure of an 
applicant’s conviction history.  Reference: Cal. Gov’t Code § 12952(a)(1).   
b. Prior to making a conditional offer of employment, there shall be no 
inquiry into or consideration of the conviction history (if any) of any 
applicant.  Reference: Cal. Gov’t Code § 12952(a)(2). 
 
(2) While conducting a conviction history background check in connection with 
any application for employment, no consideration shall be afforded to any 
of the following:  
a. an arrest not followed by conviction;  
b. referral to or participation in a pretrial or post -trial diversion program; 
and  
c. convictions that have been sealed, dismissed, expunged, or statutorily 
eradicated pursuant to law.  Reference: Cal. Gov’t Code § 12952(a)(3). 
 
(3) After a conditional offer of employment is extended, if the intent is to deny 
an applicant a position of employment solely or in part because of the 
applicant’s conviction history, an individualized assessment shall be made 
of whether the applicant’s conviction history has a direct and adverse 
relationship with the specific duties of the job that justify denying the 
applicant the position, which shall include consideration of the following:   
a. the nature and gravity of the offense(s) or conduct;  
b. the time that has passed since the conviction for a criminal offense(s) or 
conduct and/or completion of any related sentence; and  
c. the nature of the job held or sought.  Reference: Cal. Gov’t Code § 
12952(c)(1)(A), subds. (i)-(iii). 
 
(4) If, after undertaking the individualized assessment described above , a 
preliminary decision is made that an applicant’s conviction history 
Vons (Carrillo) 000118
Case 8:25-cv-00191-SRM-KES     Document 55-1     Filed 01/14/26     Page 190 of 191 
Page ID #:1015
2 
 
disqualifies the applicant from employment, written notification shall be 
provided to the applicant of this preliminary decision (the “Pre -Adverse 
Action Letter”), which shall include the following information: 
a. notice of the disqualifying conviction or convictions that are the basis for 
the preliminary decision to rescind the job offer;  
b. a copy of the conviction history report, if any;  
c. an explanation of the applicant’s right to respond  to the notice of the 
Company’s preliminary decision before the decision becomes final, and 
the deadline by which to respond; and  
d. notice that the applicant’s response to the preliminary decision may 
include submission of evidence challenging the accuracy of the 
conviction history report that is the basis for rescinding the job offer, 
evidence of rehabilitation and evidence of mitigating circumstances, or 
both.  Reference: Cal. Gov’t Code § 12952(c)(2), subds. (A)-(C). 
 
(5) If a final decision is made to deny an applicant employment solely or in part 
because of an applicant’s conviction history, written notification shall be 
provided to the applicant (the “Adverse Action Letter”) of the following:   
a. the final denial or disqualification;  
b. any existing procedure f or the applicant to challenge the decision or 
request reconsideration with Company; and  
c. the applicant’s right to file a complaint with the Department of Fair 
Employment and Housing regarding the final decision.   Reference: Cal. 
Gov’t Code § 12952(c)(5), subds. (A)-(C). 
The Company also will comply with applicable local laws, including local laws in the 
City of Los Angeles and the City of San Francisco. 
Nothing in this standard operating procedures document is intended to impose 
obligations on the Company beyond or greater than what applicable law specifically 
requires. 
Vons (Carrillo) 000119
